**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–5 (`formatWhen`).
- Produces:
  - `LEDGER_KIND_COPY` (session "Chat started", auth "Credential check", verify "Identity check", decide "Action checked", review "Second look by Claude", breaker "Chat paused", approval "Your answer", execute "Action ran", payment "Payment", document_link "Document link", reply "Reply checked", policy "Rules changed", tool_pin "Tool definition", takeover "Take over", whisper "Whisper", rate_limit "Rate limit", agent_auth "Agent signature", head_checkpoint "Record checkpoint", desk_data "Desk details changed"); `ORIGIN_COPY` (kernel "Recorded by your Desk", api "Reported by the chat, not verified by your Desk"); `CATEGORY_COPY` (Plan 02b Task 10's `Triage.category`: prices "Prices and discounts", records "Someone's records", codes "Verification codes", instructions "Instructions in a message", limits "Above a limit", wording "The AI's wording", other "Other"); `stoppedBy(a)` (`reply_blocked` → `reply`, else `action`); `entryFor(a, page)` (the entry with `seq === a.ledger_seq`, else null); `entryTime(entry)`, the one display time of a ledger entry, `entry.event.summary.replayed?.at ?? entry.created_at`, formatted with `formatWhen` wherever an entry's time shows, the signed entry itself left unchanged.
  - `SafetyPage` (N-Safety): "Safety"; serif "I turned down {n} requests this week." ("I turned down 1 request this week." for one, and "I didn't turn down any requests this week." once both reads are in and nothing was turned down; "this week" as the owner signed it; `n` is `today.this_week.stopped`, the count Today's "Last 7 days" shows, which counts only the requests the kernel refused, never Claude's own drafts the reply check stopped (decision, 2026-10-31, #216 and #220); `EmptyState` "Nothing stopped this week" only once the attempts list has been read, while neither it nor the count has failed since its last good read, and with no refused request or stopped draft in the week; with stopped drafts alone, the "didn't turn down any" headline, and the list shows them); "{k} held for you" → Today; `ChainStatus` (`chain_ok`, `first_bad_seq`, `entries.length`, `devKey` = `health.kernel?.dev_signing_key ?? false`); `SegmentedControl` "Attempts" / "Every entry" (`?view=entries` opens "Every entry", which Today's "Includes recorded history" links to; an entry whose `event.summary.replayed` exists (`{at, recording}`, inside the signed event; plan 01d, Friday) says "Recorded history, replayed into your copy" and shows `entryTime`, its `summary.replayed.at`, never `created_at`, which is when the kernel wrote it into this copy; entries without it show no label). Attempts: `Chip` filters, "All" first and selected by default, then one per category in `CATEGORY_COPY` order (Plan 02b Task 10), then "Not sorted yet"; then `AttemptRow`s (`time` = `formatWhen(created_at)`, `triage?.summary ?? reason_plain`, `who` = `ORIGIN_COPY[origin]`, `stoppedBy`, detail link). Every entry: `RecordList` rows (seq, kind, `ORIGIN_COPY[origin]`, `formatWhen(entryTime(entry))`) opening `FullTrace`.
  - Provenance everywhere: every attempt row, record row and attempt detail says where its entry came from in `ORIGIN_COPY`'s words, "Recorded by your Desk" or, for chat-service entries, "Reported by the chat, not verified by your Desk", and nothing else ("From chat" and "From the chat service" are gone). The full trace keeps the technical words ("Recorded by the checkpoint."), as the owner chose; the page passes it nothing new.
  - Times: every attempt and record time comes from the shared `formatWhen`: "Today, 9:52 am" for today's, the date and time for older ones ("Oct 26, 9:52 am").
  - `AttemptDetail`: `StatusLabel` "Stopped" (held "Held for you") and the category; serif `reason_plain`; its `ORIGIN_COPY` line and `formatWhen` time; a refused attempt adds "This action didn't run." and a reply-blocked one "This reply was never shown." (each claim covers only the stopped item: earlier actions in the chat keep their own receipts); the triage summary labelled "Summary written by Claude"; "See how it was stopped" → `CheckpointMeter`; "Full trace" → `FullTrace` for `entryFor` with chain and dev-key flag ("This entry isn't in the record yet. Try again in a moment." when missing); "Open conversation" when the entry names a session.

The demo path (both projects): the intro dialog's passcode and "Start the demo" → "You're Mike Turner today", with no chat open and "Your public page" and "Last 7 days" in the right column; "Chat as a customer" opens the public page in a new tab (`context.waitForEvent('page')`); there, "How much is drain clearing?" → a "Quote" card with a receipt under it; "Can I get 50% off if I pay cash?" → a held receipt and "Cancel this request"; back on Today, a new approval card beside the seeded hold (mock 5 s, live 60 s) and the chat under "Live now"; "Allow this time" → its final line, and the public tab shows a resolved receipt; Safety → "No entry has been changed since it was written"; axe (WCAG 2.2 AA tags) on Today, the public page and Safety → none. Live mode asserts structure, not wording; no approval card in 60 s fails with "The model didn't hold the discount; check the conversation in the owner app", never retried in a loop. The path's cross-tab flows (the hold reaching Today, the answer reaching the public tab) run on the shared mock (Task 3b: one copy in the dev server's process for every tab of the browser context) and on the live API; screen tests stay on one page.

It happened before you arrived.") and the footer has no `TakeOverBar` or composer, since no one is on the other side of a recorded chat.

The API nulls it once it has passed and the screen polls every 2 s, so the browser's clock is never compared with it; a newer `visitor_text` row replaces the bubble. Nothing else shows it: the screen never guesses that someone is typing.
  - `useOwnerTyping(sessionId)`: while the owner types in the "Write to them" composer in mode `owner`, `ownerTyping {generation}` (the last read `state.generation`) on the first keystroke, then at most once every 2 s while typing goes on. Nothing in `ai` mode (a whisper never reaches the visitor), in `paused` or for an empty composer, and a send ends it. A 409 (`take_over_first`, or a changed generation) stops the pings and refetches the conversation with no line, since typing is presence, not a message; any other failure is dropped.

One helper, `ownerTypingDelayMs(ev, nowMs)` in `chatReducer.ts`, gives `Date.parse(ev.until) - nowMs`, capped at 5000 (the API's 5 s), a result that isn't a finite number counting as 0. The provider calls it with `Date.now()` for each `owner_typing` event: at 0 or less the event is stale and shows nothing (one replayed after a reload or a reconnect included); otherwise the reducer's `owner_typing {name}` sets `ownerTyping`, and a timer of that delay clears it. The owner's `message` (author `owner`) clears it in the same dispatch, so his words take the dots' place; `owner_left`, `turn_end`, an `error` and a new chat clear it too, and so does the timer at `until`; a later `owner_typing` restarts the timer. It shows as `WritingIndicator` `variant="person"` with `who` = the event's `name` at the end of the transcript (his initials, his name and three dots where his words will start, with no bubble; the component says "{name} is typing" once to screen readers), never as Claude writing. `.until` is read only in `ownerTypingDelayMs` and `openStatusDelayMs`, and no `new Date`, `Intl` or time formatting enters `src/public/` (Task 1's guard).
  - `useVisitorTyping(slug)` in `PublicChat`: while the visitor types in the composer with non-empty text and a session open, `visitorTyping` on the first keystroke, then at most once every 2 s; nothing for an empty composer, a send ends it, and every answer and failure is dropped (a 401 is left to the send path's session-ended line).

The query key is `['decision', decision_id]`, read once and never polled. `OwnerTranscript` passes `reason` to `MarginReceipt` (design PR #43), and `cardBelow` on a decision's first `held` receipt when that request's `ApprovalCard` is drawn right after its row, so the receipt reads "Held for you" there, "Held for you. {reason}" for a hold with no card under it (one already answered), and "{OWNER_STATE_WORDS[state]}. {reason}" otherwise ("Automatic. …" for an action the rules ran on their own). Every other receipt keeps the event's `visitor_plain` and makes no read, so a receipt after a hold never repeats the hold's words ("Mike Turner approved this" keeps the kernel's line), and an allowed action refused at execution never shows the reason it was allowed. While the read is pending, or after it fails, there is no `reason` and the receipt shows the event's `visitor_plain`. ### Task 9: Rule drafts: review and confirm

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/owner/rules/{RuleDraftReview.tsx,drafts.ts,changeRows.ts,checkStatus.ts,ruleWords.ts}`, `web/src/owner/connectors/catalog.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/lib/format.ts` (`hoursParts`, `formatHours`, `listWords`), `web/src/app/routes.tsx` (`rules/drafts/:version`), `web/src/api/{routes.ts,views.ts}` (`confirmTool`'s batch body, typed by hand until the regenerated types have it), `web/src/api/routes.test.ts` (`confirmTool`'s body in the copied table), `web/src/mocks/served.ts` (routes `confirmRules`, `ruleDraft`, `people`, and `confirmTool` with the batch body: turning on drafts one version for the listed tools, turning off applies at once with no draft, and turning off `native.start_verification` is refused, as the kernel refuses it)
- Test: `web/src/owner/rules/drafts.test.ts`, `web/src/owner/connectors/catalog.test.ts`, `web/src/owner/rules/drafts.screen.test.tsx`, `web/src/api/routes.test.ts`, `web/src/lib/format.test.ts`

Owner decisions, 2026-10-29 (the signed N-Conn-10-GCal-Confirm, N-Conn-18-Preset-Review and N-Conn-20-Ceiling-Review): the change review shows each change's Before and After beside its bars; a preset's review lists every rule it removes; the payment limit reads in the signed words everywhere, setup included.

Owner decision, 2026-10-30 (the owner signed every board): where this task's text differed from the signed N-Conn-18-Preset-Review and N-Conn-20-Ceiling-Review, the boards win. The rule words are the boards', a side names its target only when the row's target moves, a shared rule is titled by the tools it covers and listed with them, and a preset's review adds a second check line. The payment limit's review shows "Rules / Change your payment limit" above its title, as a preset's shows "Rules / Ask me first": the owner chose this crumb on 2026-10-30, departing from N-Conn-20, which draws "Rules" alone (Review Ledger, 2026-10-30).

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–5; the design system's `ChangeReview` (`RuleChangeDiff` renamed, PR B, with its Before and After bars) and its `ChangeSide`, `ChangeRowView` and `ruleDiff`; `LimitChange` and `ChangeReview.cancelsLinks` (the follow-up PR; "Before you start"); `ConnectorView`, `OUTCOME_WORDS`, `PRESET_WORDS`, `RulesPreset`, `rulesForTool`, `Breadcrumb`, `Notice`, `PageHeader`.
- Produces:
  - `CONNECTORS` in `connectors/catalog.ts`, the web's static connector table (the API groups no tools; plan 02b Task 9): in order, the built-in `quotes` (`native.quote`), `bookings` (`native.find_slots`, `native.book`), `leads` (`native.capture_lead`, `native.request_callback`), `verification` (`native.start_verification`), `documents` (`native.release_document`) and `payments` (`native.payment_link`, `native.charge_mandate`), then the external `stripe` (the mock payments server's `payments.list_charges` and `payments.create_refund`, always with its Mock badge) and `google_calendar` (catalog only: no tools, no route, no API). Each key is the design system's connector id, and each entry carries its `ConnectorView` (name, icon, description and source, in the words of N-Conn-17-Presets and N-Conn-23-Connectors). `TOOL_NAMES: Record<ToolName, { name; line }>` in the boards' words ("Quote", "Find open times", "Book a visit", "Save a lead", "Request a callback", "Confirm it's them", "Share a document", "Payment link", "Charge within a signed limit", "See charges", "Refund", and the setup-only "Website reading" and "Memory reading", which belong to no connector; `line` says what the tool does, "Prices a job from your price list."). `connectorOf(tool)`; `connectorByKey(key)`, null for an unknown key. Rules, Connectors, Today and setup group tools only through it (Tasks 12–14).
  - `toolState(row)` in `catalog.ts`, what a `ToolRow`'s status means on every screen (plan 01c's tool lifecycle), so no screen reads `status` alone:

    | `ToolRow` | `toolState` | Shown as |
    |---|---|---|
    | `enabled` | `on` | "On", and its outcome on Rules |
    | `needs_review` with `pending_sha256` (its definition changed) | `changed` | "Needs your review" on Rules, Today's changed-tool item, and `ToolReviewCard` on its page (Task 14) |
    | `needs_review` with no pending hash (connected and never turned on, or no longer listed by its connector) | `not_on` | "Off", with no Today item; its page offers the add flow (Task 14) |
    | `disabled` | `off` | "Off" |

    A connector is on (a section on Rules; "Built in" or "Connected" on Connectors) while any of its tools is `on` or `changed`, else off. Only `ToolReviewCard`'s "Turn back on" sends a `pending_sha256`, the hash of the definition the owner just read; every other turn-on (setup, the add flow) sends the row's `definition_sha256` and leaves out any tool that carries a pending hash (Tasks 12 and 14).
  - `confirmTool`'s batch body, `{tools: [{tool, definition_sha256}], enable}`: a whole connector in one action, one tool a list of one (plan 02b Task 7, pending). Tasks 12 and 14 use it.
  - `RuleDraft = { version; before; after; proof: ProofReport | null; summary: RulesDraft['summary'] | null; moneyCap: { before: number; after: number } | null; preset: RulesPreset | null; turnedOn: ToolRow[] }`. `moneyCapChange(activeCap, draftCap)` gives `{before: activeCap, after: draftCap}` when they differ, else null; the active version's cap is the cap in force (master "Money cap"), so the comparison is always with what applies until the owner confirms, in either direction and after a reload. In the three builders `active` is `RulesView.active`, the active `PolicyVersion`, and each sets `moneyCap` as `moneyCapChange(active.auto_money_cap_cents, <the draft's or version's auto_money_cap_cents>)`: `draftFromAnswer(active, cardDiff, draft)` (before = active cards whose `card_id` is in the diff); `draftFromDiff(draft, active, preset?)` (before = removed + each `changed` pair's first; after = added + each pair's second; a null `diff` → `draft.cards` as added; `preset` when a preset drafted it, Task 13, which only titles the review); `draftFromVersion(active, version: PolicyVersion, turnedOn)` (a `ToolConfirmed.draft`; non-default cards; `turnedOn` the rows of the tools the confirm named). `is_default` cards never appear. A tool's draft (`turnedOn` non-empty) shows only inline on the route that made it, the add flow's Confirm step or the tool page (Tasks 12 and 14), never at `rules/drafts/:version`, since `RulesDraft` doesn't say which tools it turns on: a reload there starts that flow again with nothing applied. Every card draft rebuilds from `RulesDraft` alone, so a reload shows the same rows.
  - `changeRows(draft, people, tools): ChangeRowView[]` in `changeRows.ts`, the rows the screen passes to `ChangeReview` (the design system decides nothing); `tools` is this Desk's catalog, the `rules` tool rows with their effects:
    - A row per card from `ruleDiff(draft.before, draft.after)`, keyed by `card_id`. Its `title` is the tool's `TOOL_NAMES` name for a tool card. A shared rule (an effect card, such as `effect:charge`) is titled by joining the names of the tools it covers in this Desk's catalog with `listWords` ("Payment link and Charge within a signed limit"), whether or not they share a connector. A tool is covered as the master's "Coverage and overlap" says, read through `rulesForTool` over the effect cards on the card's side of the draft, so an `auto` rule on a tool with several effects covers it only jointly. Only a shared rule that covers none of this Desk's tools keeps its group's words ("Every tool that charges money", "Every tool that sends money out", "Every tool that touches private data", "Every tool that sends a message out", "Every tool that changes something", "Every tool that only reads").
    - Coverage follows a rule's outcome and the shared rules beside it, so a shared rule changed in place can cover other tools than it did: an automatic rule on private data for record owners covers only See charges, and made "Never" for anyone it covers every tool that touches private data. A row whose two sides are shared rules covering different tools is titled, given its connector and placed by every tool either side covers, in review order ("Share a document, See charges and Refund"), and counts as a moved target (below).
    - `connector` is `connectorOf(tool)` for a tool card, and for a shared rule the one connector all the tools it covers share, else none (the component names a built-in connector alone and an external one with its Mock badge).
    - The rows go by connector, as the Connectors page lists them, and by tool within one: the changed rows, then the added ones. A shared rule with a connector sits right after the rows of the first tool it covers, as N-Conn-18 draws it between Payment link and Charge within a signed limit; one whose tools span connectors, or that covers none, goes last. The draft's cards reach `reviewChanges` in the draft's order, and only the rows are sorted.
    - `shared: true` (the design system's `ChangeRowView.shared`, plan 03b, on `main`) is set on every row whose rule is on a group of tools on each side it has: a shared rule dropped to the default ask, changed in place (whether or not the tools it covers change), added, or replaced by another group's rule, as the shared rule for Alex Kim's assistant goes to "Recommended rules"' rule for every tool that charges money. Its subline reads "{connector} · Shared rule" ("Payments · Shared rule"), or "Shared rule" leading when it has no connector. A row whose rule moves between one tool and a group of tools leaves it unset, since its sides' `appliesTo` show the move, and so does every `turned_on` row.
    - Each side is `{ appliesTo?, mode, limit, reason, words }`. `appliesTo` is what the side's card applies to, in words, on both sides, only when the row's target moves: a card retargeted, or a removed card going to a draft card on another target, gives each side's target (the tool's name or the group's words); a shared rule that covers other tools after than before gives each side the tools it covers ("See charges", then "Share a document, See charges and Refund"), or its group's words when it covers none of this Desk's; for a rule moved between two groups, the tools each side covers win over its group's words ("Payment link and Charge within a signed limit", then "Refund"). So a widened or narrowed target shows. Otherwise the row's title names it and the sides don't repeat it (N-Conn-18). `limit` is `formatCents(max_amount_cents)` for an auto card with one; `words` come from `ruleWords(card, people)`. `changed` is the diff's fields, with `applies_to` added when only the coverage moves, so the after side's target is tagged.
    - The rows come from the design system's `reviewChanges(draft.before, draft.after, effectsOf)` (`effectsOf` from the tool rows' effects), the one algorithm every review's rows follow (plan 03b Task 3c), and `changeRows` only gives each side its words:
      - a card the draft keeps by its `card_id` is a `changed` row where it differs;
      - a card the draft removes, personal ones included, goes to the draft's card that covers its whole scope (its people, days and hours) among those that govern its target (`rulesForTool`, most specific first; the cards on the same effect for an effect card), as a `changed` row tagged where they differ, or no row when the pair differs only in its `card_id`;
      - with no covering card it is a `changed` row whose `after` is the default side `{ mode: 'ask', reason: 'Not covered by a rule you set, so I ask you.', words: [{ field: 'who', text: 'Anyone' }] }`, with `changed` `['mode']` only when the mode changes, so no row claims an outcome the draft doesn't enforce for all of the rule's scope;
      - a draft card no removed card goes to is an `added` row; one a removed card goes to is that row's After;
      - a personal rule's words name the person ("Dana Whitfield").
      On "Ask me first" (N-Conn-18-Preset-Review) nothing in the preset covers what it drops, so each dropped rule reads as a change to "Ask me first"; "Recommended rules" leaves quotes without a row under its own identical quote rule.
    - Each `turnedOn` tool adds a `turned_on` row: its name, its connector, and as `after` the outcome Rules shows for the draft's rules (`toolOutcome`, Task 13's note of 2026-10-31), in the words of the rule that gives it, else the default side.
  - `cancelsLinksFor(draft)` in `changeRows.ts`, whether the review says open payment links may be cancelled (master "Cancellation at activation": the kernel cancels each open link Claude made on its own that the version being activated no longer allows), read from the draft's cards and cap, never from where the draft came from: true when the cap goes down, when a card on `native.payment_link` or `effect:charge` that is `auto` before is gone or changed after (in anything but its reason or a higher `max_amount_cents`), or when the draft adds any card on either target. A raise alone, or changes to other targets, never set it.
  - `hoursParts(w: HoursWindow): { days: string; times: string; allDay: boolean }` in `format.ts`, a window in the boards' words, with `allDay` true exactly when it runs from midnight to midnight, so Settings needn't compare `times` with its words (Task 14):
    - `days`: "Weekdays" (Monday to Friday), "Weekends", "Saturdays", "Sundays", "Every day"; any other set by plural day names, Monday first ("Mondays, Wednesdays and Fridays"), three or more in a row as a range ("Monday to Thursday"), and weekdays and weekends by name beside other days ("Weekdays and Saturdays", "Mondays and weekends").
    - `times`: "8:00 am to 6:00 pm"; an end at 00:00 is "midnight", the day's end, and midnight to midnight is "all day".
    - `formatHours(w)` is "{days}, {times}" ("Weekdays, 8:00 am to 6:00 pm", "Saturdays, 9:00 am to 1:00 pm"). Settings joins each window's parts with a space and no comma after the days ("Weekdays 8:00 am to 6:00 pm, Saturdays 9:00 am to 1:00 pm", Task 14).
  - `ruleWords(card, people, agents?): RuleWord[]` (Plan 03's `{field, text}`, the words `ChangeReview`, `RuleRow` and `ConnectorReview` show), in the boards' words, the same as the design system's `rivera.ruleWords` for every Turner card:
    - `who`: "Anyone", or "Signed agents" on `native.charge_mandate`, which only an agent with a signed limit can use; "Verified customers"; "The record's owner, verified", or "The customer who paid, verified" on a refund (`payments.create_refund`, `effect:pay_out`); a person's `display_name` ("Dana Whitfield"; "One customer" while `people` loads or fails), then "Verified" on a tool that touches private data; an agent's name from `agents` by `agent_key_id` ("Alex Kim's assistant"), else "One agent", since the API names no agent yet.
    - `limits`: "Up to {formatCents}", "Discounts up to {max_discount_bp / 100}%", "No discounts" on a quote rule that allows none.
    - `hours`: `formatHours`.
    - `review`: "Checked by the reviewer".
  - `checkStatus(proof, preset?)` (only `checked` can confirm; `ProofReport` per master "How proofs run"), passed to `ChangeReview` as `checks` (`status` and `lines`):

    | Proof | Kind | Copy |
    |---|---|---|
    | `null`, `timed_out`, or `method: 'bounded'` | unavailable | `RULES_UNAVAILABLE_COPY` |
    | `ok`, `symcc` | checked | "Checked against the always-on rules and your other rules."; on a preset's review (`preset` set, either preset) also "Open times, leads, callbacks and identity checks still run on their own." (N-Conn-18) |
    | not `ok`, not `timed_out` | failed | "This change can't be applied:" then each check's `counterexample_plain` (or "One of the safety checks didn't pass.") |

  - The payment limit's words, the signed ones everywhere (N-Conn-19-Ceiling, N-Conn-19b-Ceiling-Edit, N-Conn-20-Ceiling-Review), each meaning the most Claude takes or pays out in one action without asking, whatever the rules say (spec "Autonomy"); "up to $X without asking you" and setup's "Most I take without asking" are retired: `moneyCapWords(cents)` "no payment over {formatCents} without asking you" ("no payment over $1,000 without asking you"), at 0 "every payment asks you first"; `moneyLimitLine(cents)` "No payment over {formatCents} goes through without asking you, whatever the rules below say.", at 0 "Every payment asks you first." (Rules, Task 13, and setup's rules step, Task 12); `moneyLimitHint(cents)` "Whole dollars, from 0 to 10,000. Now {formatCents}.", at 0 "Whole dollars, from 0 to 10,000. Now every payment asks you first." `PAYMENT_MAX_CENTS = 1_000_000`, the per-payment limit (the baseline's `money_has_amount`) and the platform ceiling `AUTO_MONEY_HARD_CAP_CENTS`, so no money limit can be set above it (master "Money cap").
  - `RuleDraftReview({ deskId; draft; title?; inline?; onDone?; confirm? })`:
    - Unless `inline` (the Ask Claude panel, setup), a `PageHeader` titled `title` (the caller's: a preset's `PRESET_WORDS` label, "Change your payment limit", "Turn on {connector}"), else "Change your payment limit" when only the limit changes, else "Review the change", with "Here's exactly what changes on your Desk. Nothing changes until you confirm." and a `Breadcrumb` back to where the change started (Rules, or Connectors for a connector or tool turned on once the Connectors pages exist, through `connectorPageHref`, and Rules until then, Task 13's note of 2026-10-31), ending with the title ("Rules / Ask me first", and "Rules / Change your payment limit" for a change to the payment limit alone, the owner's choice of 2026-10-30 where N-Conn-20 draws "Rules" alone).
    - When `moneyCap` is set, first `LimitChange` with `now` "New limit: {moneyCapWords(after)}." and `was` "Was {formatCents(before)}", for a raise or a lowering alike, so a typo shows before confirming and nothing changes until "Confirm change".
    - The summary's three lines in serif when present, then `ChangeReview`: `rows` from `changeRows`; `checks` from `checkStatus(draft.proof, draft.preset)`, so confirm is enabled only when `checked`; `hasOtherChanges` when `moneyCap` is set, so a draft that only raises or lowers the limit, with no card changing, reads "Your rules stay the same." and can still be confirmed; `cancelsLinks` from `cancelsLinksFor(draft)` (the line "Open payment links I made on my own that the new rules don't allow will be cancelled."); `confirmLabel` "Confirm and turn on" with `cancelLabel` "Back" when `turnedOn` is non-empty.
    - "Confirm change" → `confirm` when given (setup's `setupConfirm`), else `confirmRules {version}` → "Your rules are updated." with a link to Rules; 409 `rules_changed_since_draft` → "Your rules changed since this was drafted. Ask for the change again."; a drafting route's `rules_cannot_be_checked` (409 or the proving give-up) → the unavailable line with no review; "Keep my rules" (or "Back") → back, with nothing changed. Reused by Tasks 12–14.
  - Route `rules/drafts/:version`, for card drafts only, reads router state (a `RuleDraft` and its `title`, or Task 5's card diff and `RulesDraft`), else loads `ruleDraft` and builds the same rows titled "Review the change" (404 → "This draft isn't open anymore. Ask for the change again from Rules."). A reloaded preset draft shows only the first check line, since the draft doesn't say which preset made it.

Every card is the design system's `Card` but the send panel's. The page holds one visitor session for both actions (`useAgentSession`): it calls `ensureSession(slug)` before the first `agentKey` or `sendAgent`, and a 401 opens one new session for every call that found the old one gone, retries once, then shows `CHAT_ENDED_COPY` with `NEW_CHAT_LABEL`.
  - `ConnectYourOwn`: "Connect your own agent" (`AgentCardOut.endpoint` in `CopyField` with "Copy address", its `signing` in plain lines (headers, payload fields, canonical form, algorithm, encoding, time window), `example_request` in `CodeBlock`, and "Connecting your own Claude means pointing an A2A client at this address. It isn't a ready-made connector."), then "Try it with a demo key": "Simulated in this demo: the built-in agent and its wallet stand in for someone else's. Neither key is a business credential." and "Get a demo key" → `agentKey` → key id and private key shown once in mono, "Copy key", "Shown once. It's a demo key for this Desk, not a business credential."; once the key shows, the button and the simulated line are gone (board 02).
  - `SendAgentPanel({ slug, business, ownerName, session, language })`: "Send an agent" in the design system's `Card` with `holdsCards`, since it holds the run and the Desk's reply cards. Its lead before a run is "A built-in agent visits {business} for Alex Kim. It's simulated." (the name marked as its own words); while a run is starting or exists, the chosen task takes its place. `SignedLimitPanel` from the static strings shows in every state, as board 05 draws it during a run. A `DEMO_TASK_COPY` chip → `sendAgent`, then `agentRun` each second until `state` is `done` or `failed` or the run is gone (404, or a 403, 409 or 422), with no deadline: reads slow to every 5 s after a minute, pause while the tab is hidden, stop when the page goes away, and keep going through failed reads (no answer, a 5xx or a 429), with `UNREACHABLE_COPY` after five in a row; a 401 on a read ends the chat. `agent` turns carry empty `blocks` and the request as the agent sent it, `text` (Plan 02; owner decision, 2026-11-01), and the turn is built by code: `AgentMessage` "Alex Kim's assistant", signed, in the page's language, `title` the chosen task on the first turn and "Signed request" after, `meta` "Request {n}", no `time`, and `text` the agent's request, quoted under the title inside the agent's own row and labelled as its words, never styled like the Desk's checked replies: plain text only, never markup, its whitespace collapsed and cut to 280 characters (past that, at most 279 and "…", with no space left before the "…"), marked English (`textLanguage`, the run's language) on either page. A turn without words, or with only whitespace, draws with no quote. Anything else an agent turn holds, its blocks or receipts, is ignored. `desk` turns → `ClaudeMessage` from their `blocks` with read-only `receipts`. Then "The agent finished.", with the chips again (owner, 2026-10-30), so a visitor can send the agent on another task. A failed or 404 run: "The agent stopped before finishing. Try again.", with the chips. A 429: "The demo agent is busy. Try again in a minute.", with the chips. A 503 `demo_budget`: `DEMO_BUDGET_COPY`, with the chips. Each task click sends `sendAgent {task, idempotency_key}` with a new key (`newIdempotencyKey`, a random UUID: the browser's `randomUUID`, or one from `getRandomValues` on a page where it is missing), and that click's retries (a 401's one retry in a new session, and "Try again") send the same key, so the API answers the run the click started rather than starting a second. A lost start (no answer, a timeout or a 5xx to `sendAgent`, which no read can reconcile without the run's id): the lost line (`agentStartLostLine`, the design system's `AGENT_START_RETRY_COPY`) with "Try again", which sends the same task with the same key and so picks up the run whether or not it had started, then reads it as above; no chips meanwhile. A 409 `conflict` on a first send (the key came with another task, from another session or to another Desk) means it wasn't sent: `UNREACHABLE_COPY` with the chips and the lead, and the next click makes a new key. Two lost starts can't be settled by a same-key retry, so they fall back to the reload as before the API took keys (`AGENT_START_LOST_COPY` with a `RELOAD_PAGE_LABEL` button that reloads the page, and no chips on that page view): a 409 `conflict` on "Try again" (a 401 opened a new session, and the key, with any run it started, is the old session's), and a key 55 minutes old (`KEY_RETRY_WINDOW_MS`, a margin under the hour the API keeps a send's key), checked by a timer and again on the press. Until the send itself answers a retry, the start stays unresolved and no fresh task shows: a session that can't reopen (an `openSession` refusal or no answer) keeps the lost line and "Try again", and a second 401 ending the chat is the reload too, never a new chat with its tasks. Only the API's own `conflict` means the key moved session; the kernel's 409 refused the send and freed its key, so it reads as on a first send. "Try again" that sends again moves focus to the panel's title, as a task does; past the window the reload takes the same button, and focus stays on it. Pressing a task brings the run into view, and its newest step stays in view as it plays while the visitor is at its end, smoothly or at once under reduced motion (#130).
  - Public page: `aboutLinks` "What agents can do here" (`/d/:slug/agents`) and "Send an agent" (`/d/:slug/agents#send`, the panel) under "For AI agents"; `about` stays the charter blocks.

### Task 12: Setup flow

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/setup/{SetupPage.tsx,SetupPage.css,StartStep.tsx,QuestionsStep.tsx,ToolsStep.tsx,RulesStep.tsx,SafetyStep.tsx,LiveStep.tsx,copy.ts,shared.ts}`, `web/src/owner/share/{CopyLink.tsx,CopyLink.css}`, `web/src/mocks/setup.ts` (the setup routes and a scripted seven-question setup, the money limit among them; keyed starts as the API answers them, and `holdNextStart` for a start still under way)
- Modify: `web/src/app/routes.tsx` (`/app/setup`, `/app/setup/:setupId` = `interview_id`), `web/src/mocks/served.ts` (the setup routes), `web/src/api/{routes.ts,views.ts}` (`setupAnswer`'s body `{question, text}`; the setup read models as plan 02b has them: `SetupState.consent` and `question`, `Situation`, `SafetyCheckRow.tries` and `blocked`)
- Test: `web/src/setup/setup.screen.test.tsx`

Owner decisions, 2026-10-30 (the signed N-R2-Setup-01 to 16): the rules step's payment limit is a read-only line with no "Change"; the situations step has no "Change a rule"; built-in connectors that aren't on can be added in setup, as N-R2-Setup-05 draws them.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–4, 9 (`PublicChat`, `checkStatus`, `RuleDraftReview`, `CONNECTORS`, `toolState`, `moneyLimitLine`, `ruleWords`, `confirmTool`'s batch body and its mock); the design system's `Steps`, `ConnectorReview`, `SetupToolsList`, `OutcomeGroups`, read-only `MoneyLimit` (no `onEdit`), `AlwaysOnList`, `SituationRow`, `SafetyCheckRow`, `Composer` with its failed state, `CopyField` with `useCopyText` and `copyResultLine`.
- Produces:
  - Shell: the bell with no tile at 20 px at every width (`Mark size={20}`, the mark guide's standalone-page size, as the setup boards draw it, #108) 10 px from the `Wordmark`, with its Preview badge from 1100 px up and none below (a phone's top bar carries no badge), the business name once known (it never widens its column, and a long name is cut), `Steps` "Questions, Tools, Rules, Safety check, Live" (`questions` 0, `tools` 1, `rules`/`situations` 2, `safety_check` 3, `preview`/`live` 4; one line under 480 px, "Step 1 of 5: Questions"), "Save and exit" (the new Desk's Today, or `/`), `ConceptFooter`. An interview outside the copy (404) → the not-found screen. Reads after a write whose answer was lost make one attempt. Every stage after the rules, however it is opened, reads the draft first and goes on only when `checkStatus(draft.proof)` is `checked`; otherwise the can't-check line and only "Save and exit".
  - `StartStep`: "Tell me about your business" / "A few sentences is enough. You'll see everything before anything goes live."; multiline "Your business" (`maxLength` 1500; hint "Include your first name as customers should see it.", from which Plan 02 takes `owner_display_name`); "Start" → `startSetup {description, timezone}` (the browser's IANA zone) and `idempotency_key` → `/app/setup/{setupId}`. Each press of "Start" makes a new key (`newIdempotencyKey`), and every retry of that press sends the same body with the same key, so the API answers the interview it made rather than making a second Desk (plan 02b, #124). A lost start (no answer, a timeout or a 5xx) may have made the Desk: the lost line (`setupStartLostLine`, the design system's `SETUP_START_RETRY_COPY`) with "Try again", which resends with the same key and goes on to the interview it returns. A 409 `in_progress` (the first start with that key is still running) waits its `retry_after` (2 s when it names none, scaled like the polls) and sends again with the same key, up to three times; still under way, the page says it is taking longer (`setupStartSlowLine`, the design system's `SETUP_START_SLOW_COPY`) with "Try again", the same key. "Start" stays off while a start is unresolved. A clear refusal (a 422, a 409 `conflict`, another final 4xx, or a 409 `key_spent`, which says that key can't start anything again: `setupStartSpentLine`, `UNREACHABLE_COPY` until the design system's `SETUP_START_SPENT_COPY` is approved) shows its line, "Start" works again, and the next press makes a new key; nothing is retried on its own after one. The description is read-only while a start is in flight or unresolved, since "Try again" sends it as it was, and "Try again" moves focus to the step's heading (`PageHeader`'s `titleRef`). Setup's keys live with their interviews and don't expire, so "Try again" has no time limit. A start that answers after the owner has left the page is neither cached nor followed.
  - `QuestionsStep`: messages in order (`claude` → serif `ClaudeMessage`; `owner` → `VisitorMessage`; `tool_label` → `ToolLabel`), under "Question N of 7" from `SetupState.question`; the consent question offers "Yes, use it" / "No, I'll type it" (`setupConsent`); then an owner `Composer` ("Answer in your own words", `maxLength` 2000, disabled while an answer is out) → `setupAnswer {question, text}`, where `question` is `SetupState.question`, the question the words answer, with `WritingIndicator`. The API refuses an answer to a question the interview has moved past with 409 `conflict`, so an answer sent twice never answers the next one. An answer turn can store the owner's words and ask a follow-up on the same question, and a long turn can commit after the page reads, so an answer is settled by what came after it was sent (the step records how many messages the interview held): its exact words stored among them means taken, whether or not the question moved on, and the page moves on; something else came, the question moved on or another answer was stored, means refused, and the words go back in the composer; nothing yet means unknown. A 409 to a first send is the API's refusal: the step reads the interview and the words go back in the composer for the question now asked. Words a read shows stored are never sent again or put back. A consent whose answer was lost, or a 409 (given already), reads the interview too; when that read fails, the next press reads first, so consent is never sent twice. A failure, an unknown outcome, or one the read can't settle because it failed too, uses the `Composer`'s failed state (`failed={{ text, line, onRetry }}`) with "I couldn't answer just now. Your answer is still here; try again."; "Try again" reads the interview first and settles the answer the same way, sending the same words again, with their question, only while the outcome is still unknown. While the failed box shows, nothing can be typed or sent.
  - `ToolsStep`: "What Claude can use", the new Desk's connectors from `setupTools` in the design system's `SetupToolsList` (N-R2-Setup-05): "Built in" (Quotes, Bookings, Leads and callbacks, Verification "Always on", Documents, Payments) and "Connect your own" (Stripe, the mock payments server, with its Mock badge, "Your customers' charges and refunds. Test mode, no money moves." and "2 tools"). Each row reads its tools through Task 9's `toolState`: a connector with a tool `on` or `changed` reads "On" (a `changed` tool is left out of any turn-on here; its review is on its tool page, Task 14); one whose tools are all `not_on` or `off` offers "Add" (built in) or "Connect" (Stripe). Stripe's "Connect" → `setupConnect` first. Either then opens `ConnectorReview` of its tools, each at the outcome Rules shows for the rules in force (`toolOutcome`, Task 13's note of 2026-10-31), else "Ask me first", with the rules of the owner's that cover it. "Turn it on" → one `confirmTool {tools: [{tool, definition_sha256}, …], enable: true}` for the connector's tools, each with its row's `definition_sha256`, the hash it was listed at → its `ToolConfirmed.draft` in an inline `RuleDraftReview` (`draftFromVersion` with those rows as `turnedOn`) under "Turn on {connector}" → "Confirm and turn on" → `confirmRules` → `setupTools` refetched; the tools read "On" only then (master "Activation gate"). A built-in "Add" sends the same requests as Stripe's flow, without `setupConnect`. A `rules_changed_since_draft` 409 re-runs `confirmTool` once and shows the new draft with "Your rules changed since this was drafted. Check the new version."; `rules_cannot_be_checked` → the unavailable line. "Continue" → `setupRules`.
  - `RulesStep`: "Your rules" / "What I'll do on my own, what I'll ask you about, and what I never do."; the payment limit as a read-only `MoneyLimit` with `moneyLimitLine(draft.auto_money_cap_cents)` ("No payment over $500 goes through without asking you, whatever the rules below say.", or "Every payment asks you first." at 0; the signed words, which retire "Most I take without asking") and no "Change"; then `OutcomeGroups` ("Does on its own", "Asks you", "Never does"), a row per draft card with its reason and who it is for (`ruleWords`); `AlwaysOnList`; the `checkStatus` lines. "Check real situations" → `setupSituations` → `SituationRow`s (title, who, the kernel's outcome and `depends_on_review` as they came, `reason_plain`, "Not what we planned" when `!matches`; a situation planned `allow_if_reviewed`, "does it if Claude's second look agrees", comes back a hold that depends on the reviewer, with `matches` true, as the API's `situation_matches` counts it); "Looks right" → Safety check; no "Change a rule". `rules_cannot_be_checked` (409 or the proving give-up), or a draft whose check couldn't run (bounded, timed out) → the signed can't-check line (N-R2-Setup-10), "I can't check these rules right now, so they can't go live yet. Your answers are saved; come back from Today and I'll check them again.", only "Save and exit"; a draft whose check ran and failed shows its `checkStatus` lines, what didn't pass, with only "Save and exit". The tools and situations subtitles show the business's name as the owner's own words (`data-copy="user"`).
  - `SafetyStep`: `setupSafetyCheck` → "{blocked} of {total} blocked" and "I tried {total} tricks people really use, on your draft rules. Nothing was real, and no one was contacted."; rows staggered by `--motion-stagger` (static under reduced motion): label, the quoted try (`data-copy="user"`), "Action checkpoint", outcome, `reason_plain`; "Run again" (a rerun that fails drops the earlier rows, so "Confirm rules" waits for a run that succeeds); "Confirm rules" → `setupConfirm` → `PolicyVersion` (a result that is a `RulesDraft` after a proving retry sends `setupConfirm` once more; a lost answer, or a 409 `conflict`, which the API answers once setup is past the safety check, reads the interview, which is at `preview` once confirmed; the proving give-up that hasn't moved on keeps the button with the design system's `UNREACHABLE_COPY`, "Something went wrong on our side. Try again in a moment.", which doesn't call it not done, since a repeated post joins the running work; the server's 409 `rules_cannot_be_checked` → the can't-check line, only "Save and exit"); 409 `rules_changed_since_draft` → its `draft` in an inline `RuleDraftReview` under "Your rules" / "Confirming your rules." ("Your rules changed since this was drafted. Check the new version."), with "Confirm change" and "Back" (N-R2-Setup-12): "Confirm change" sends `setupConfirm` again and settles a failure as "Confirm rules" does (through `RuleDraftReview`'s `onConfirmError`, whose own handling the Rules page keeps); "Back" runs the safety check again on the draft that will be confirmed. Answering stays off through all of it.
  - `LiveStep`, opened by a successful `setupConfirm`. Pre-live state (step `preview`): "Try it as a customer before anyone else can" / "Answering stays off until you go live. Going live reads the Desks again, so Today's Answering reads on at once. Live state (step `live`): "{business} is live" / "Customers and their agents can reach your Desk at this link, day or night."; the link with `CopyLink` and "Open"; "Go to Today"; Task 15 adds `ShareKit`. `CopyLink({ url })`: the link in `CopyField` and "Copy link" through `useCopyText`; on clipboard failure the link is selected and `copyResultLine` says "Copy didn't work. The link is selected, so copy it yourself."

nothing precedes Claude's consent question); `tool_turns_on_only_after_its_change_is_confirmed` (connect → Stripe with "Mock" and its two tools at "Ask me first"; "Turn it on" → one `confirmTool {tools, enable: true}` naming both tools, each with its row's `definition_sha256`; neither reads "On" until "Confirm and turn on" → one `confirmRules`, then both "On"); `built_in_connector_turns_on_only_after_its_change_is_confirmed` (the same for Payments' "Add", with no `setupConnect`); `tool_confirm_conflicts_fail_closed` (`confirmTool` 409 `rules_cannot_be_checked` → the unavailable line; `confirmRules` 409 `rules_changed_since_draft` → one more `confirmTool` and the new draft, then "On"); `rules_step_groups_and_checks` (with "No payment over $500 goes through without asking you, whatever the rules below say." and no "Most I take without asking"); `rules_cannot_be_checked_blocks_go_live` (`setupRules` 409, a bounded draft, and the proving give-up on a clock the test moves); `situations_flag_mismatches` (a planned second look isn't flagged); `resumed_setup_never_goes_on_with_unchecked_rules` (bounded and timed-out checks at the situations and the safety check show the can't-check line, a failed one its lines, and the mock refuses the confirm); `failed_safety_rerun_drops_the_old_result`; `setup_gives_the_new_business_its_own_name` (the preview, the public page, the owner's Desks, Today's greeting and its payment links name it, never Turner); `business_names_are_the_owners_words`; `safety_check_lists_outcomes` ("6 of 6 blocked"); `confirm_then_preview_then_go_live` (`setupConfirm`, then `openPreview`, then `goLive`, in that order, with no `goLive` before `setupConfirm` succeeds; the preview's disclosure while Answering is off); `confirm_conflict_shows_the_new_draft` (`setupConfirm` 409 with a `draft` → its review; "Confirm change" → one more `setupConfirm`, then the pre-live state); `confirm_conflicts_and_give_ups_are_read_not_called_failed` (a lost confirm, then a 409 → the preview; the give-up on a clock the test moves → the button stays; the server's 409 → the can't-check line); `setup_bar_shows_the_preview_badge_from_1100_px` (the 20 px bell with no tile at both widths; the badge at 1100 px and none at 1099; "Save and exit" the same width on one line, and the lockup clear of the steps); `setup_fits_phone_width` ("Save and exit" on one line at 390 and 320 px, a long business name cut with an ellipsis); `setup_has_no_jargon`; `tools_start_at_the_outcome_rules_shows` (2026-10-31: with rules for verified customers on Payment link, for weekday hours on Charge within a signed limit and for record owners on Share a document, each review starts the tool at "Automatic", the outcome Rules shows for the same rules).

### Task 13: Rules page: outcomes by connector, Ask Claude, presets and the payment limit

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/owner/rules/{RulesPage.tsx,RuleChat.tsx,HouseRules.tsx,askClaude.tsx,hub.ts}`
- Modify: `web/src/app/routes.tsx` (`rules`, `rules/all`), `web/src/owner/OwnerLayout.tsx` (passes the open Ask Claude panel to `AppShell`'s `panel`), `web/src/api/routes.ts` (the `draftMoneyCap` key), `web/src/api/routes.test.ts` (the new row in the copied table), `web/src/mocks/served.ts` (routes `ruleChat`, `suggestions`, `draftSuggestion`, `draftPreset`, which replaces every owner card except the Desk's own jobs' cards (enabled, or awaiting review of a changed definition) that move no money (`charge` or `pay_out`), keeps those cards unchanged unless a preset card has the same ID, and lists each removed card in `diff.removed` (owner, 2026-11-02), and `draftMoneyCap`, which changes nothing until `confirmRules` activates the draft, as the kernel does), `web/src/mocks/readModels.ts` (`preset` on `rules`, `ask_first`, `auto` or null, ignoring those eligible non-money job cards (owner, 2026-11-02); each tool row's `cards`), `web/src/mocks/world.ts` (Turner's active version at `auto_money_cap_cents: 50000`; Dana's personal booking rule and the demo agent's `rv-agent-charges`, for tests to add)
- Test: `web/src/owner/rules/hub.test.ts`, `web/src/owner/rules/rules.screen.test.tsx`, `web/src/api/routes.test.ts`

Owner decisions, 2026-10-29 (the signed N-Conn-17-Presets, N-Conn-19-Ceiling, N-Conn-19b-Ceiling-Edit, N-Conn-20-Ceiling-Review and N-Conn-22-Phone-Presets; spec "Autonomy"): Rules holds outcomes only, grouped by connector, each group linking to its connector page under Connectors (Task 14; until those pages exist, without a link, by the note below). The Ask Claude composer sits under the header. "Start from a preset" replaces the mode switch: there is no mode and no "Custom", and each of its two items drafts a preset as one bulk edit that opens the proved change review. Applying a preset replaces every owner card except the Desk's own jobs' cards (enabled, or awaiting review of a changed definition) that move no money (`charge` or `pay_out`), which stay as they are; `RulesView.preset` ignores those job cards. A preset card wins if its ID collides with a job card's ID (owner, 2026-11-02). Other person-scoped and `never` cards are replaced, and the review lists each card it removes. The payment limit's line sits at the top of the Payments and Stripe groups, worded as the most Claude takes or pays out in one action without asking. No row is labelled "Built in", and outcomes read "Automatic", "Ask me first" and "Never".

Connector links, 2026-10-31 (#187): the connector and tool pages come with Task 14, and until they exist nothing links to them, so no link opens the not-found page. `CONNECTOR_PAGES_BUILT` (`web/src/owner/connectors/pages.ts`) stays off until then, and `connectorPageHref(deskId, path)` gives no address while it is. So the Rules headers and tool rows (`ConnectorSection` and its `ToolSummaryView`s with no `href`) and the All rules tool lines (`ToolRulesCard`'s `tool` with no `href`) draw the same words with no link, no chevron and no tab stop; the sidebar has no Connectors row (no `hrefs.connectors`); and a review that turns tools on starts its trail at Rules, not Connectors (`RuleDraftReview`, Task 9; `turn_on_review_leads_back_to_rules_until_connectors_are_built` in `drafts.screen.test.tsx`). The sidebar already marks Connectors as the current row on a Connectors page. `hubSections` keeps each `href`. Screen tests turn the switch on with `renderRoute`'s `connectorPages` option, which resets after each test.

Tool outcomes, 2026-10-31 (#159): a tool's one outcome is the most that runs on its own for some visitor, so it never reads safer than what runs. Rules (`hubSections`), the change review's `turned_on` rows (`changeRows`, Task 9) and setup's starting outcomes (`ToolsStep`, Task 12) read it from one function, `toolOutcome` (`web/src/owner/rules/outcome.ts`), so the three agree. It follows the kernel's rule compiler (`backend/desk/kernel_service/compiler.py`) for each visitor the rules tell apart, each with the rules for them: a stranger (rules for anyone); a verified customer (rules for anyone and for verified customers); a record's owner (those and rules for record owners), only on a tool whose records can have an owner; each person a rule names, a verified customer with the rules for verified customers and their own, read both as the owner of the record a request is about and as someone else's customer where records can have an owner, since which records are theirs is the kernel's to know; and each agent a rule names, with its own rules on top of those of each standing an agent's session can have (a session is verified once it is bound to a person, `sessions.py`), a named person's included. "Allow only for this person, always" drafts such a rule at the held amount, so what runs for that person counts. The catalog's `TOOL_RESOURCES` says what each tool acts on, as the kernel's tool profiles do. Record ownership is read as the kernel's adapters set a resource's `owner_person_id`: only document and charge resources have an owner (a document's `record_owner_person_id`; a charge's person), and a desk, slot or quote resource has none (the adapters for the Desk's own tools and for booking set `owner_person_id=None`, and the quotes table has no owner column). So only Share a document, See charges and Refund have record owners. This assumes plan 01c's payment link and signed-limit charge adapters give a quote no owner; if one did, `OWNED_RESOURCES` would change with it. For one visitor, a never rule on the tool or on one of its effects refuses; otherwise an automatic permit runs unless an ask rule at its rank or above, or another permit above it, covers the tool, ranked as the kernel's `specificity` ranks rules: one for a person or agent above one for a group, then a tool's own above a shared one; a reviewer's check counts as passed; anything else asks. A permit is one automatic rule on the tool or on its only effect, or, on a tool with several effects, one automatic shared rule per effect together, all at one rank. Limits: a permit runs up to the tightest amount limit among its rules, so shared rules on a tool's several effects all hold; the permits that run add up, so a visitor's limit is the largest of theirs; and the outcome takes the largest across visitors and times. The limit shown is the rules' own; the payment limit has its own line. Hours are read exactly: only an automatic rule has hours, so the week is cut at every edge of every rule's hours and each stretch between two edges is read on its own. The outcome is the most permissive across visitors and stretches of the week (Automatic, then Ask me first, then Never), at the largest limit, and where readings tie it names the stranger's rule. So a never or ask rule for verified customers, or for one person or agent, leaves a tool automatic for strangers; Dana Whitfield's own rule up to $450 beside Payment link's $300 for anyone reads "Automatic" up to $450; a never rule for record owners on Quote, which is no one's record, refuses no one; and a shared never rule for anyone still reads "Never". The always-on baseline's rules that change what runs for one of these visitors are read too: private data goes only to the verified person the record belongs to (`private_data_record_owner`), so a tool that touches it runs, if at all, only for the record's owner, and Share a document, automatic for anyone beside a never rule for record owners, reads "Never"; an agent never pays money out (`no_agent_pay_out`), so an agent's automatic Refund rule runs for no one; and money moves only in an amount above zero (`money_has_amount`), so an automatic money rule up to $0 runs for no one. The baseline's other rules change nothing these visitors can run, as `outcome.ts` sets out rule by rule. No tool in the Turner seed, and none that setup offers, reads differently. `hub.test.ts` adds `summary_is_the_most_that_runs_for_some_visitor`, `summary_is_never_only_when_every_visitor_is_refused`, `record_owner_rules_count_only_where_records_have_owners`, `summary_limit_is_the_kernels`, `summary_holds_over_the_week`, `private_data_goes_only_to_the_record_owner`, `named_people_and_agents_are_visitors_too`, `agent_rules_run_for_their_agents`, `a_named_rule_never_reads_stricter_than_the_group` and `money_moves_only_in_an_amount_above_zero`; `drafts.test.ts` adds `turned_on_rows_read_as_rules_does`; `catalog.test.ts` adds `record_owners_only_where_the_kernel_resolves_one`; and `setup.screen.test.tsx` adds `tools_start_at_the_outcome_rules_shows`.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1–5 (`aboveMoneyCapCopy`), 9 (`ruleWords`, `RuleDraftReview`, `draftFromDiff`, `CONNECTORS`, `TOOL_NAMES`, `toolState`, `moneyLimitLine`, `moneyLimitHint`, `PAYMENT_MAX_CENTS`); the design system's `PageHeader`, `PresetMenu`, `PRESET_WORDS`, `Composer`, `SuggestionChips`, `ConnectorList`, `ConnectorSection`, `ToolSummaryView`, `MoneyLimit`, `AlwaysOnList`, `AskClaudePanel`, `RuleRow`, `rulesForTool`, `OUTCOME_WORDS`, `describeEffects` and `AppShell`'s `panel`.
- Produces:
  - In `hub.ts`:
    - `governingRules(row: ToolRow, active)`: `rulesForTool({ tool, effects }, active.cards)`, the rules that govern the tool under the master's "Coverage and overlap" (person- or agent-scoped cards first, then tool cards, then effect cards; a `never` card always shows; defaults left out), not every covering card. So the demo agent's `rv-agent-charges` (an agent-scoped `effect:charge` card, up to $20) comes first under Payment link and Charge within a signed limit. `ToolRow.cards` says which of them are shared (effect-group cards).
    - `toolRuleRows(row, active, people, tools): RuleRowProps[]`, for the connector and tool pages (Task 14): one per governing rule, with `words` from `ruleWords`, `limit` for an auto card with `max_amount_cents`, and `shared` for an effect card, its words naming the group and the covered tools among `tools`, the Desk's tool rows ("Applies to every tool that charges money: Payment link and Charge within a signed limit."). `tools` is required (2026-10-31, #159), so no caller drops the covered tools' names; the fallback row unless a `never` card for anyone covers the tool.
    - `withOutcome(card, mode, row, capCents): RuleCard | { error: string }`, the card an outcome menu drafts (Task 14), valid under the `RuleCard` contract: to `ask` or `never`, the card with its auto-only fields cleared (`max_amount_cents` and `hours` null, `max_discount_bp` 0, `review` false); to `auto`, the card as it is, except that a card with no `max_amount_cents` on a tool whose effects include `charge` or `pay_out`, or on `effect:charge` or `effect:pay_out`, takes the Desk's money cap as its limit, which the review shows before anything applies; at a cap of 0 it drafts nothing and returns `aboveMoneyCapCopy(0)` ("You haven't set a limit for taking money without asking. Set one first.").
    - `hubSections(view: RulesView, people): { connector; href; tools: ToolSummaryView[]; moneyLimit: boolean }[]`: one per connector that is on (`toolState`, Task 9), in `CONNECTORS` order (a connector turned off leaves Rules and Connectors' lists and comes back through the picker, Task 14), and never Google Calendar, `native.fetch_website` or `native.read_memory`. `href` is `connectors/{key}`. Each tool's `ToolSummaryView` takes `name` and `description` from `TOOL_NAMES` (a connected tool's line ends with `describeEffects`); `summary` from its governing rules ("Anyone · No discounts" for one rule; "{n} rules", then " · {k} shared" when some are shared; "Always on" for Confirm it's them; "No rule yet" when none); `mode` and `limit` from `toolOutcome`, the most that runs on its own for some visitor (the tool outcomes note above); `status` from `toolState` (`changed` as `needs_review`, `not_on` and `off` as `disabled`); and `href` `connectors/{key}/{encodeURIComponent(tool)}`. `moneyLimit` is true for `payments` and `stripe`.
    - `parseLimit(text): { cents } | { error }`: whole dollars only (`^\d+$`); above `PAYMENT_MAX_CENTS` → "No single payment can be above $10,000."; anything else that isn't whole dollars from 0 to 10,000 → "Enter whole dollars, from 0 to 10,000."
  - `RulesPage` (`rules`, N-Conn-17-Presets; N-Conn-22-Phone-Presets at 390 px):
    - `<PageHeader title="Rules" subtitle="What Claude can do on this Desk, where each tool comes from, and your rules for it." actions={<PresetMenu …/>} actionsBelowOnPhone hideSubtitleOnPhone />`: at a laptop's width the menu sits at the right of the title's line (N-Conn-17-Presets); under 640 px it takes a line of its own 16 px under the title, the button and its menu at the column's full width, and the subtitle isn't shown (N-Conn-22-Phone-Presets).
    - Under the header, the owner `Composer` ("Ask Claude to change a rule", helper "Claude shows you the change and example situations before anything applies.", `maxLength` 2000) with `SuggestionChips` "Stop quoting on weekends", "Quote water heater installs up to $600" and "Why was Dana's refund held?"; sending opens the Ask Claude panel (`RuleChat`, below). These words replace N-Conn-17's "…or a connector" and "Turn off Payments", by the project owner's decision (Open question 5), so the page never promises an action Ask Claude can't take.
    - Suggestion cards when `suggestions` has any ("Draft this rule" → `draftSuggestion` → the draft review page).
    - "Version {n} of your rules, checked against the always-on rules", with "See all rules" → `rules/all`.
    - `ConnectorList` of `hubSections`' `ConnectorSection`s, each header and tool row linking to its page through `connectorPageHref` (no link until Task 14, by the note above), with no row labelled "Built in" (its add action, while the component keeps one, opens the picker at `connectors/add`, Task 14); `MoneyLimit` at the top of the Payments and Stripe sections (below); then `AlwaysOnList`.
    - `RulesView.preset` is provenance only and never drafts or changes anything. It would tick the matching item, but `PresetMenu` marks none (the signed N-Conn-17-Presets and the follow-up's `no_selected_state` play), so the page passes nothing from it until the design system adds one (Open question 6).
  - "Start from a preset": `PresetMenu`, whose items come from `PRESET_WORDS` ("Ask me first" → `ask_first`, "Recommended rules" → `auto`, with the signed descriptions, as N-Conn-17 draws them; the spec calls the first "Ask me first for everything"). `onPick(preset)` → `draftPreset {preset}` (`pending` while it runs) → `rules/drafts/{version}` with router state `draftFromDiff(draft, active, preset)` and the preset's `PRESET_WORDS` label (N-Conn-18-Preset-Review): every rule the preset removes, personal ones included (`RulesDraft.diff.removed`, plan 02b), in Task 9's rows (from `reviewChanges`: a changed row to the preset's rule that covers the dropped rule's whole scope, else a changed row to "Ask me first"; a preset rule nothing goes to is an added row), the cancellation line above "Confirm change" when `cancelsLinksFor` says so, and nothing changes until it is confirmed.
  - The payment limit (`MoneyLimit`, N-Conn-19 and N-Conn-19b): the same cap in both groups, one field open at a time. `line` is `moneyLimitLine(active.auto_money_cap_cents)`; "Change" opens the field (its default label "Most a payment can be without asking you, in dollars", `hint` `moneyLimitHint(cap)`). "Review change" (or Enter) runs `parseLimit`: an `error` shows in the field and nothing is sent; the current amount closes the field with no request; any other amount, higher or lower → `draftMoneyCap {auto_money_cap_cents}` (`disabled` while it runs) → `rules/drafts/{version}` titled "Change your payment limit" (N-Conn-20-Ceiling-Review: `LimitChange`, "Your rules stay the same." when no card changes, and the cancellation line only on a lowering), whose "Confirm change" applies it. A 422 `above_platform_ceiling` shows the ceiling line in the field. "Cancel" closes it.
  - `RuleChat` inside `AskClaudePanel` (N-Conn-12-Chat; `AppShell`'s `panel` beside the page, a modal sheet under 640 px), opened through `useAskClaude()` from `askClaude.tsx`: the owner's words as `PersonMessage` "You", then `ruleChat`; `answer` as serif text; a `draft` (or a bare `RulesDraft` after a proving retry) → an inline `RuleDraftReview`; the panel's composer for another change, `busy` while a request runs; "Close" and Escape return focus to what opened it; `rules_cannot_be_checked` → the unavailable line, no review; 422 `above_money_cap` → `aboveMoneyCapCopy(err.capCents)` (Task 5); other failures → "I couldn't draft that just now. Try again." `draftPreset`, `draftSuggestion` and `draftMoneyCap` handle failures the same way, each with that copy for a 422 `above_money_cap`. `RuleChatOut` carries no connector to offer, so the panel shows no `ConnectorOfferCard` (Open question 5), and adding a connector goes through the picker (Task 14). Task 14's tool pages open the same panel.
  - `HouseRules` (`rules/all`): every rule grouped by connector and tool, each tool's name and line linking to its tool page through `connectorPageHref` (no link until Task 14), as read-only `RuleRow`s with `OUTCOME_WORDS` ("Automatic", "Ask me first", "Never") and `ruleWords`, and for a tool no rule covers "Not covered by a rule you set, so I ask you."; `AlwaysOnList`. Settings keeps General, Knowledge, Notifications, Channels and Billing and names no connectors. People read "Connectors", never "Integrations". Google Calendar is catalog only in the demo: `ConnectorDirectory`'s coming-soon row ("Coming soon in this demo", no Connect), as the picker says, with no connect call. Verification is always on.

Build order (the master's "Sat 18:00" checkpoint): first what carries the tool-change story, which never turns static: the connector and tool pages with their rules and outcome menus, the changed-tool review with the simulate strip, Today's changed tools and turning a connector off; then the Connectors page, adding a connector (picker, sign-in, Google Calendar), recent requests and Settings.     - `turnOff(rows)` → `enable: false` with each row's `pending_sha256 ?? definition_sha256`, which applies at once with no draft; it never lists `native.start_verification`, and a refusal shows `ownerErrorCopy` and changes nothing.

      | `state` | Outcome | Tone |
      |---|---|---|
      | `allowed` | "Running" | info |
      | `executed` after an allow | "Automatic" | handled |
      | `held` | "Waiting for your answer" | held |
      | `approved`, or `executed` after a hold | "You allowed it" | handled |
      | `denied` | "You denied it" | refused |
      | `expired` | "Expired" | info |
      | `cancelled` | "They cancelled it" | info |
      | `refused` | "Stopped" | refused |
      | `failed`, `refused_at_execution` | "Didn't run" | refused |
      | `unknown` | "Still checking what happened" | info |

  - `KnowledgePage` (Settings › Knowledge, part B, N-Run2-Settings-Knowledge): "Knowledge is what I answer from. It never changes your rules." in Claude's serif; then "What I know now" (`KnowledgeList`): a fresh Turner copy starts with its website and price list knowledge, both public, dated at copy seeding in the order the kernel stores them (website first); the items `GET …/knowledge` returns, oldest first, each "Added {date}" on the business's clock (`formatDate`; a null or unreadable `created_at` leaves the date line out) and "Customers can see this" or "Only you" from its returned readers; an item whose `title` or `text` comes back null, one the kernel forces owner-only, reads "A note only you can see", with no title, no body and no switch. The list says "Loading what I know" while it is read, says what went wrong with "Try again" over what was read before, is left out while nothing is known, and is read again after each add or change, cancelling any read already in flight before reading fresh. Then "Add something" (`KnowledgeAdd`): "Add a note" (`addKnowledge` with `item_id` `note-<uuid>`, source `setup`, title at most 60 characters, shortened at a word with "…", readers `["owner"]`; an empty note → "Write the note first, then add it.") and "Add a file" (`.txt`/`.md` up to 100 KiB of UTF-8 text, decoded with `new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true })` from `file.arrayBuffer()`, source `upload`, `item_id` `file-<uuid>`, titled by the file's name; larger → "That file is too big. Add files up to 100 KB.", another kind or an empty file says so, and none sends anything). After a confirmed add (answered or read back as present), the note clears only if its current trimmed words still match what was posted; words typed while it was in flight stay. Files and notes are capped by UTF-8 byte length at 100 KiB. An oversized note reuses "That didn't go through. Check it and try again.": the file-specific too-large line does not fit a note. Invalid UTF-8 or NUL in a file reuses "We couldn't read that file. Pick it again." Then "Added. Only you can see it until you make it public." and a "Customers can see this" `Switch` re-posting the item with `["public"]` or `["owner"]`. The switch shows the readers in the `KnowledgeItem` the answer returns, never the ones asked for, so an item the kernel keeps owner-only stays off, with "This stays private. Only you can see it." An add or a change whose answer is lost, or a 5xx, may have gone through: the page reads the list again, with a read sent after the change (one already in flight is cancelled), before it gives the controls back and shows the item as the list has it, readers included ("That wasn't added. Add it again." when the list doesn't have it); if the list can't be read either, the outcome stays unknown ("couldn't confirm"), the switch stays where it was, and adding the same thing again posts it under the same id, which replaces it rather than adding a second. Once an add is confirmed, its retry id is cleared: deliberately adding the same words creates a new item and cannot un-publish the first. A lost publish read back as owner-only uses "This stays private. Only you can see it." only when the row's title is withheld (`title === null`); otherwise it reuses "That didn't go through. Check it and try again." Writes go one at a time: while one is out, the switch and both adds are disabled and do nothing; only the control whose write is out is announced as busy, so no answer lands on another item's card. A file the browser can't read says so. The mock mirrors the kernel's readers, record owner, id, title, storable text, byte and item caps, returning 422 `{error: "kernel", code: "invalid"}` for a kernel refusal.
  - Document links (from the kernel verification fixes, plan 01b Task 6c, and plan 02b's Task 3b): a card's document link (`/api/d/{token}`, single use) is never prefetched or unfurled. The web opens it only from the card's own link, which the visitor clicks (Task 10); nothing under `web/src` or in `web/index.html` prefetches, preloads or prerenders a link (no `rel="prefetch"`, `"preload"`, `"modulepreload"`, `"prerender"` or `"dns-prefetch"` on one, none of React DOM's `prefetchDNS`, `preinit`, `preloadModule` or `preinitModule`, no speculation rules), no screen fetches `/api/d/`, and the share kit's texts and copies carry only the Desk's public link, never a document link, so no messaging app unfurls one.

### Task 16: Generate the API's types and check every route against them

The web owns its client: `ROUTES` and `request()` (Task 1). There is no `client.ts` from the API lane: the owner dropped its generated TypeScript client (master "Shared seams", "API HTTP contract", owner decision 2026-10-29), so this task generates only the API's own types from its OpenAPI document and checks the route table against it.

**Split, 2026-10-30.** The task lands in two halves. The generator half lands first, while the API serves only part of `ROUTES`: `gen-api.mjs`, `openapi.gen.ts`, `routes.typecheck.ts` and the widened contract test. `routes.typecheck.ts` keeps two lists, which the typecheck and the contract test both hold to: `NOT_YET_SERVED`, the routes the API doesn't serve yet, each of which must still be missing from the document, so the list can only shrink; and `NEVER_CALLED`, the operations the API serves that no route calls (a document link, the A2A endpoint), each of which must be served and called by no route. The rest of the task, the `views.ts` migration, `views.typecheck.ts` and the `mocks/validate.ts` line, comes once the API's remaining routes and the regenerated contracts from the kernel verification and owner data plan (plan 01b) are on `main`, and it is done when `NOT_YET_SERVED` is empty.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/scripts/gen-api.mjs`, `web/src/api/openapi.gen.ts` (its output), `web/src/api/routes.typecheck.ts`, `web/src/api/views.typecheck.ts`
- Modify: `web/package.json` (script `gen:api`, `node scripts/gen-api.mjs`, with `--check` as `gen:contracts` has), `web/src/api/views.ts` (aliases only API-owned shapes from `openapi.gen.ts`; contract types come from `contracts.ts`; the shapes typed by hand for run 2, `EvOwnerTyping`, the `replayed` flags, `typing_until`, `ToolRow.cards`, `ToolDecisions`, `PaymentLinkView.business_name` and `customer_name`, give way to the generated ones as they land), `web/src/api/routes.contract.test.ts` (Task 7's, widened), `web/src/mocks/validate.ts` (`owner_typing` validated against the schema once `contracts.schema.json` has it)
- Test: the contract test, the typecheck files

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the API's `backend/desk/api/openapi.json` (plan 02a, from its first task); `json-schema-to-typescript` (already pinned for `gen:contracts`, so no new dependency).
- Produces: `openapi.gen.ts`, generated: the API-owned schemas from `components.schemas` as types, and `API_OPERATIONS`, each path and method in `paths` with its 2xx JSON schema's name; `request()` unchanged, with the same signature and `ApiError` mapping; compile-time checks that every `ROUTES[k].path` and method exist in `API_OPERATIONS`, and that each assumed shape is mutually assignable with its generated schema.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests:** `every_route_exists_in_openapi` (from `openapi.json`, never skipped: every `ROUTES` method and path exists; each 2xx JSON schema names the model in `ROUTE_MODELS: Record<RouteKey, string | null>`); the two typecheck files.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run** `cd web && npm run gen:api && npx vitest run --project unit src/api/routes.contract.test.ts && npm run typecheck` → FAIL wherever a name or shape differs.
- [ ] **Step 3: Reconcile** `routes.ts`, `views.ts` and the handlers to the API's document (screens change only for renamed fields). A behavior-changing mismatch goes to the run ledger as a ruling.
- [ ] **Step 4: Run** `cd web && npm run gen:api -- --check && npm run gen:contracts -- --check && npm run typecheck && npm test && npm run e2e:mock` → PASS (all projects green; 1 e2e test).
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** `git add web && git commit -m "refactor(web): generate the API's types from its OpenAPI document and type-check every route against them"`.

## Open questions for the master plan

1. **Owner reads.** Master views exist for knowledge and notification preferences, and the knowledge route is now served; notification preferences remain interim static Notifications. The API serves the knowledge list (`GET …/knowledge`, #286): Task 15's Knowledge page reads it using `KnowledgeRow` from `openapi.gen.ts`, with the mock serving the same shape.
2. **Preview before go-live.** Answered: setup confirms with `setupConfirm` (Answering stays off), the owner tries the Desk on an `openPreview` session, which gets the visitor tools while Answering is off, and `goLive` turns Answering on (Task 12).
3. **Plan 03 props.** Answered: Plan 03a owns `PublicShell.footer` and `PublicShell.aboutLinks`, `Composer.maxLength` and `TakeOverBar.agent`; this plan consumes them.
4. **Visitor typing.** Answered: plan 02b Task 1 sends typing both ways (`visitorTyping` and `OwnerTranscript.typing_until` for the owner's bubble; `ownerTyping` and `EvOwnerTyping`, by master amendment, for the visitor's view of the owner), each throttled to one call in 2 s (Task 8).
5. **Connector offers in the chat.** Answered (the project owner, 2026-10-30): new words, and no connector action in the API. `RuleChatOut{answer, draft}` names no connector, so the Ask Claude panel shows no `ConnectorOfferCard` ("Review and add"), and the web never reads one out of Claude's prose; adding a connector goes through the picker (Tasks 13 and 14). N-Conn-17's composer helper ("Ask Claude to change a rule or a connector") and first chip ("Turn off Payments") promised a connector change the panel can't make, since turning a connector off is `confirmTool`, not a rule draft. So the Rules composer reads "Ask Claude to change a rule", with "Stop quoting on weekends" as its first chip (Task 13).
6. **The preset menu's tick.** `RulesView.preset` is meant to tick the matching "Start from a preset" item, but the signed N-Conn-17-Presets and the design system's `PresetMenu` mark no item (its `no_selected_state` play). Until the design system adds a current item, Task 13 passes nothing from `RulesView.preset`, which stays provenance only.
7. **Tool pages behind the hosted edge.** Resolved (coordinator's ruling, 2026-10-29): the hosting edge's `spa_rewrite` (plan 06b) serves `/index.html` for every `/app/*` path, since the owner app has no files under `/app` and assets stay at `/assets/`, and keeps its no-dot rule elsewhere, so a tool page's dotted last segment (`connectors/stripe/payments.create_refund`, Task 14) reloads and shares on the hosted site. The route stays as settled.

Ruling, 2026-10-31, from the review rounds, signed off by the project owner, on "Try to break it" typing its idea into the public page (it used to be copied for the owner to paste): the public page types in only the three fixed ideas in `web/src/lib/breakItIdeas.ts`, handed over by this browser's Today through its own storage under a one-time token (`?try=<token>`), and never words from the address (Tasks 3 and 4). What the hand-off guarantees: only the owner's own Today, in this browser, can type one of the three fixed lines, once for each pick; a link on its own, from anyone, types nothing. Free text in a link (`?draft=<text>`) is rejected: any link to a Desk's public page could otherwise pre-type a third party's words in a visitor's own chat, focused and one keypress from being sent, and the record would show the customer as having said them. An idea's key in the link (`?try=<key>`) is rejected too: the fixed list narrows the words but not who can plant them, so a link sent to a real customer would still put an attack line in their box, one Enter from putting them on record as the attacker. A `?preview` gate, accepting free text only on the owner's preview link, is rejected as well: `?preview` is a query anyone can add to a link, and for anyone but the Desk's owner it falls back to a visitor's chat, so it says nothing about who is reading. An unknown or used token, more than one `try`, or a `draft` alone types nothing, and each leaves the address; a `draft`'s words are ignored and removed, so a valid `try` beside one still types its idea.
